# McLaren

This article is about the racing team. For the parent company, see [McLaren Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Group). For the car manufacturer, see [McLaren Automotive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Automotive). For other uses, see [McLaren (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_\(disambiguation\)).

For this team's complete IndyCar Series history, see [Arrow McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrow_McLaren).

**McLaren Racing Limited** ([/məˈklærən/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*mə-KLARR-ən*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key)), currently competing in [Formula One](10854.md) as **McLaren Mastercard F1 Team**, is a British [motor racing](1022.md) team based at the [McLaren Technology Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Technology_Centre) in [Woking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woking), Surrey, England. The team is a subsidiary of the [McLaren Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Group), wholly owned by [Mumtalakat Holding Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumtalakat_Holding_Company).&#x20;

McLaren is best known as a Formula One chassis [constructor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_constructors), the second-oldest active team and the second-most successful Formula One team after [Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Ferrari), having won 205 races, 13 [Drivers' Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_Drivers%27_Championship), and 10 [Constructors' Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_constructors%27_championship). McLaren is one of only three constructors, and the only team, to complete the [Triple Crown of Motorsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_Crown_of_Motorsport#Teams_and_manufacturers) (wins at the [Indianapolis 500](159018.md), [24 Hours of Le Mans](1401596.md), and [Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monaco_Grand_Prix)). The [2026 Chinese Grand Prix](80284337.md) marked McLaren's 1000th Grand Prix entry, making them the second constructor after Ferrari to reach this milestone. The team currently uses [Mercedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_AMG_High_Performance_Powertrains) power. Its partnership with [Honda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_in_Formula_One) from [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Formula_One_World_Championship) to [1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_Formula_One_World_Championship) produced four consecutive Constructors' Championships; the team won 15 of 16 races in 1988.&#x20;

Founded by [Bruce McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_McLaren) in 1963, the team made its Grand Prix debut at the [1966 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Monaco_Grand_Prix), won its first Grand Prix at the [1968 Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Belgian_Grand_Prix), and took its first Formula One championships in [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Formula_One_season). [Emerson Fittipaldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerson_Fittipaldi), [James Hunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Hunt), [Niki Lauda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niki_Lauda), [Alain Prost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alain_Prost), [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna), [Mika Häkkinen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mika_H%C3%A4kkinen), [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton), and [Lando Norris](48978813.md) have won Drivers' Championships with McLaren. Champions [Denny Hulme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denny_Hulme), [Jody Scheckter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jody_Scheckter), [Keke Rosberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keke_Rosberg), [Fernando Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Alonso), [Kimi Räikkönen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimi_R%C3%A4ikk%C3%B6nen), and [Jenson Button](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jenson_Button) also drove for the team at one point during their careers. The team's most recent titles were secured in [2025](72768531.md).&#x20;

McLaren also has a history in [sports car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_car_racing) and [endurance](699018.md) racing, with a [modified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_F1_GTR) [McLaren F1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_F1) [road car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Automotive) winning the [24 Hours of Le Mans](1401596.md) in [1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_24_Hours_of_Le_Mans). The team is set to reenter the [FIA World Endurance Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_World_Endurance_Championship) in 2027 with an [LMDh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LMDh) entry, the [McLaren MCL-HY](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL-HY). The team has also had experience in [American open wheel racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_open_wheel_racing) as both an entrant and a chassis constructor, and has won the [Canadian-American Challenge Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Can-Am) (Can-Am) [sports car racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_car_racing) championship. Their current [IndyCar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IndyCar_Series) entry, [Arrow McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrow_McLaren), has won 10 races since its takeover of [Sam Schmidt Motorsports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Schmidt_Motorsports) in [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_IndyCar_Series). McLaren has previously entered the [electric motorsports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_motorsport) [Formula E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_E) and [Extreme E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_E), though they achieved less success there than their other ventures and ended up pulling out of both. McLaren also participates in [Formula One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_Esports_Series) [esports](564204.md), in which they won the [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Formula_One_Esports_Series) championship.&#x20;

## Background

Black-and-white photograph of Bruce McLaren

The McLaren Racing team's founder [Bruce McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_McLaren)

Bruce McLaren Motor Racing was founded in 1963 by New Zealander [Bruce McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_McLaren).[^cite_note-mclarenhistory-9] Bruce was a [works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Factory-backed) driver for the British [Formula One](10854.md) team [Cooper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooper_Car_Company) with whom he had won three [Grands Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prix_motor_racing) and come second in the [1960](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1960_Formula_One_season) World Championship. Wanting to compete in the [Australasian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australasia) [Tasman Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasman_Series), Bruce approached his employers, but when team owner Charles Cooper insisted on using 1.5-litre Formula One-specification engines instead of the 2.5-litre motors permitted by the Tasman rules, Bruce decided to set up his own team to run him and his prospective Formula One teammate [Timmy Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timmy_Mayer) with custom-built Cooper cars.[^cite_note-10]&#x20;

Bruce won the [1964 series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Tasman_Series), but Mayer was killed in practice for the final race at the [Longford Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longford_Circuit) in [Tasmania](29944.md). When Bruce McLaren approached Teddy Mayer to help him with the purchase of the Zerex sports car from Roger Penske, Teddy Mayer and Bruce McLaren began discussing a business partnership resulting in Teddy Mayer buying in to Bruce McLaren Motor Racing Limited (BMMR) and ultimately becoming its largest shareholder.[^cite_note-11][^cite_note-mayerobituary-12]&#x20;

The team, [competing under a British racing licence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_constructors#Team's_nationality),[^cite_note-13] was based in [Feltham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feltham), England, from 1963 to 1964, and in [Colnbrook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colnbrook), England, from 1965 until 1981, and it has been based in [Woking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woking), England, since 1981.[^cite_note-14] Despite this, Bruce never used the traditional [British racing green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_racing_green) on his cars. Instead, he used colour schemes that were not based on [national principles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_auto_racing_colors#Major_competitors) (e.g. his first [Formula One car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_car), the [McLaren M2B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M2B) car, raced at the [1966 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Monaco_Grand_Prix), was painted white with a green stripe, to represent a fictional Yamura team in [John Frankenheimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Frankenheimer)'s film *[Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prix_\(1966_film\))*).[^cite_note-15]&#x20;

During this period, Bruce drove for his team in [sports car races](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_car_racing) in the United Kingdom and North America and also entered the [1965 Tasman Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1965_Tasman_Series) with [Phil Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Hill), but did not win it.[^cite_note-16] He continued to drive in Grands Prix for Cooper, but judging that team's form to be waning, decided to race his own cars in 1966.[^cite_note-henry18-17]&#x20;

## Racing history: Formula One

### Overview

The team began life as a [Formula One constructor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_constructors) in 1963 by [Bruce McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_McLaren). The team's maiden Grand Prix victory was at the [1968 Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Belgian_Grand_Prix). After Bruce McLaren died in a testing accident in 1970, [Teddy Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teddy_Mayer) took over and led the team to their first Formula One Constructors' Championship in [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Formula_One_season), with [Emerson Fittipaldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerson_Fittipaldi) and [James Hunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Hunt) winning the Drivers' Championship in 1974 and [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Formula_One_season) respectively. The year 1974 also marked the start of a long-standing sponsorship by the [Marlboro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marlboro_\(cigarette\)) cigarette brand.&#x20;

In 1981, McLaren merged with [Ron Dennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Dennis)' [Project Four Racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Four_Racing); Dennis took over as team principal, and shortly afterwards organised a buyout of the original McLaren shareholders to take full control of the team. This began the team's most successful era; with [Porsche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porsche_in_motorsport) and [Honda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_in_Formula_One) engines, [Niki Lauda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niki_Lauda), [Alain Prost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alain_Prost), and [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna) won seven Drivers' Championships between them and the team took six Constructors' Championships. The combination of Prost and Senna was particularly dominant – together they won all but one race in [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Formula_One_World_Championship) – but later their rivalry soured and Prost left for Ferrari. Fellow English team [Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Grand_Prix_Engineering) offered the most consistent challenge during this period, the two winning every constructors' title between [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Formula_One_World_Championship) and [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_Formula_One_World_Championship). By the mid-1990s, Honda had withdrawn from Formula One, Senna had moved to Williams, and the team went three seasons without a win. With [Mercedes-Benz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_AMG_High_Performance_Powertrains) engines, [West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_\(cigarette\)) sponsorship, and former Williams designer [Adrian Newey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_Newey), further championships came in [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Formula_One_World_Championship) and [1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Formula_One_World_Championship) with driver [Mika Häkkinen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mika_H%C3%A4kkinen), and during the 2000s the team were consistent front-runners, with [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) winning the [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Formula_One_World_Championship) drivers' title.&#x20;

Ron Dennis retired as McLaren team principal in 2009, handing over to long-time McLaren employee [Martin Whitmarsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Whitmarsh). At the end of 2013, after the team's worst season since 2004, Whitmarsh was ousted. McLaren announced in 2013 that they would be using [Honda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_in_Formula_One) engines from 2015 onwards, replacing Mercedes-Benz.[^cite_note-18] The team raced as McLaren Honda for the first time since 1992 at the [2015 Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Australian_Grand_Prix). The second Honda partnership did not yield good results, and in September 2017, McLaren announced they had agreed on an engine supply with [Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_in_Formula_One) from 2018 to 2020. McLaren returned to using Mercedes-Benz engines from the 2021 season, with a deal in place until at least 2030.[^cite_note-Mercedes2021-19][^cite_note-\:2-20] After a period of time in the midfield, McLaren surged to fourth in the Constructors' Championship in [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship) after having started that year on the back foot. They would go on to win their ninth Constructors' Championship, and first since 1998, in [2024](72504121.md). The [following year](72768531.md), [Lando Norris](48978813.md) took the team's most recent drivers' title, and first since 2008, and secured their tenth Constructors' Championship title and their second consecutive title; the first time this had happened since [1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_Formula_One_World_Championship). This result made McLaren the second-most successful Formula One team of all time in terms of Constructors' Championships won.&#x20;

### 1966–1968: Early beginnings

Circular logo featuring a kiwi bird

McLaren's original logo was designed by [Michael Turner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Turner_\(illustrator\)) and featured a [kiwi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiwi_\(bird\)) bird, a [New Zealand](4913064.md) icon.

[^cite_note-21]

[^cite_note-22]

White-and-green McLaren M2B on display

The [McLaren M2B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M2B), the team's first Formula One car

Bruce McLaren made the team's Grand Prix debut at the [1966 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Monaco_Grand_Prix) (of the current Formula One teams, only [Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Ferrari) is older).[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-mclarenhistory-9] His race ended after nine laps due to a terminal oil leak.[^cite_note-McLaren_Trust-26] The [1966](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Formula_One_season) car was the [M2B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M2B) designed by [Robin Herd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_Herd), but the programme was hampered by a poor choice of engines: a 3.0-litre version of [Ford](30433662.md)'s [Indianapolis 500](159018.md) engine and a [Serenissima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Serenissima) [V8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V8_engine) were used, the latter scoring the team's first point in [Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_British_Grand_Prix), but both were underpowered and unreliable.[^cite_note-henry18-17][^cite_note-McLaren_Trust-26] For [1967](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967_Formula_One_season) Bruce decided to use a [British Racing Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Racing_Motors) (BRM) [V12 engine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V12_engine), but due to delays with the engine, was forced initially to use a modified [Formula Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Two) car called the [M4B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M4B) powered by a 2.0-litre BRM V8, later building a similar but slightly larger car called the [M5A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M5A) for the V12.[^cite_note-McLaren_Trust-26] Neither car brought great success, the best result being a fourth at [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Monaco_Grand_Prix).&#x20;

### 1968–1982: Ford-Cosworth DFV engines

For [1968](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Formula_One_season), after driving McLaren's sole entry for the previous two years, Bruce was joined by 1967 champion and fellow New Zealander [Denny Hulme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denny_Hulme), who was already racing for McLaren in [Can-Am](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Can-Am).[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-clockworkorange-28] That year's new [M7A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M7A) car, Herd's final design for the team, was powered by [Cosworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosworth)'s new and soon to be ubiquitous [DFV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosworth_DFV) engine[^cite_note-29][^cite_note-tremaynehughes223-228-30] (the DFV would go on to be used by McLaren until 1983) and with it a major upturn in form proceeded. Bruce won the [Race of Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_of_Champions_\(Brands_Hatch\)) at the [Brands Hatch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brands_Hatch) circuit and Hulme won the [International Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_BRDC_International_Trophy) at [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstone_Circuit), both non-championship races,[^cite_note-31] before Bruce took the team's first championship win at the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Belgian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-32] Hulme also won the [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Italian_Grand_Prix) and [Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Canadian_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix later in the year, helping the team to second in the Constructors' Championship. Using an updated 'C' version on the M7,[^cite_note-henry24-33] a further three podium finishes followed for Bruce in [1969](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969_Formula_One_season), but the team's fifth win had to wait until the last race of the 1969 championship when Hulme won the [Mexican Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969_Mexican_Grand_Prix). That year, McLaren experimented with [four-wheel drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four-wheel_drive) in the [M9A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M9A), but the car had only a single outing driven by [Derek Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derek_Bell_\(auto_racer\)) at the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969_British_Grand_Prix); Bruce described driving it as like "trying to write your signature with somebody jogging your elbow".[^cite_note-34]&#x20;

McLaren M7A on track at the Nürburgring

The [McLaren M7A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M7A) of 1968 gave McLaren their first [Formula One](10854.md) wins. It is driven here by [Bruce McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_McLaren) at the [Nürburgring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%BCrburgring) in 1969.

The year [1970](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_Formula_One_season) started with a second-place each for Hulme and Bruce in the first two Grands Prix, but in June, Bruce was killed in a crash at [Goodwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goodwood_Circuit) while testing the new M8D Can-Am car.[^cite_note-henry24-33] After his death, [Teddy Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teddy_Mayer) took over effective control of the team;[^cite_note-mayerobituary-12] Hulme continued with [Dan Gurney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Gurney) and [Peter Gethin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Gethin) partnering him. Gurney won the first two Can-Am events at Mosport and St. Jovite and placed ninth in the third, but left the team mid-season, and Gethin took over from there. While [1971](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1971_Formula_One_season) began promisingly when Hulme led the opening round in [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_South_African_Grand_Prix) before retiring with broken suspension,[^cite_note-35] ultimately Hulme, Gethin (who left for BRM mid-season,[^cite_note-henry26-36]) and [Jackie Oliver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackie_Oliver) again failed to score a win. The 1972 season saw improvements though: Hulme won the team's first Grand Prix for 2+1⁄2 years in [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_South_African_Grand_Prix) and he and [Peter Revson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Revson) scored ten other podiums, the team finishing third in the Constructors' Championship. McLaren gave [Jody Scheckter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jody_Scheckter) his Formula One debut at the [final race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_United_States_Grand_Prix) at [Watkins Glen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watkins_Glen_International).[^cite_note-henry26-36] All McLaren drivers used the Ford-Cosworth engines, except for [Andrea de Adamich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_de_Adamich) and [Nanni Galli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanni_Galli) who used engines from [Alfa Romeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfa_Romeo_in_Formula_One) in 1970.&#x20;

#### 1973–1981: Hunt–Lauda era and transition

Fittipaldi driving a red-and-white McLaren M23

[Emerson Fittipaldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerson_Fittipaldi) won the 1974 Drivers' Championship with McLaren.

The [McLaren M23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M23), designed by Gordon Coppuck, was the team's new car for the [1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Formula_One_season) season.[^cite_note-henry26-36] Sharing parts of the design of both McLaren's Formula One [M19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M19A) and Indianapolis M16 cars (itself inspired by [Lotus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Team_Lotus)'s [72](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotus_72)),[^cite_note-37] it was a mainstay for four years.[^cite_note-38] Hulme won with it in [Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Swedish_Grand_Prix) and Revson took the only Grand Prix wins of his career in [Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_British_Grand_Prix) and [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Canadian_Grand_Prix). In [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Formula_One_season), [Emerson Fittipaldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerson_Fittipaldi), world champion with Lotus two years earlier, joined McLaren.[^cite_note-fittipaldi-39] Hulme, in his final Formula One campaign,[^cite_note-40] won the [Argentinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Argentine_Grand_Prix) season-opener; Fittipaldi, with wins in [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Brazilian_Grand_Prix), [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Belgian_Grand_Prix) and [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Canadian_Grand_Prix), took the Drivers' Championship. It was a close fight for Fittipaldi, who secured the title with a fourth at the season-ending [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_United_States_Grand_Prix), putting him three points ahead of Ferrari's [Clay Regazzoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clay_Regazzoni). With Hulme and multiple motorcycle world champion [Mike Hailwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Hailwood), he also sealed McLaren's first Constructors' Championship. The year [1975](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975_Formula_One_season) was less successful for the team: Fittipaldi was second in the championship behind [Niki Lauda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niki_Lauda). Hulme's replacement [Jochen Mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jochen_Mass) took his sole GP win in [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Spanish_Grand_Prix).&#x20;

At the end of 1975, Fittipaldi left to join his brother's [Fittipaldi/Copersucar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fittipaldi_Automotive) team.[^cite_note-fittipaldi-39] With the top drivers already signed to other teams, Mayer turned to [James Hunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Hunt), a driver on whom biographer [Gerald Donaldson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerald_Donaldson) reflected as having "a dubious reputation".[^cite_note-41] In [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Formula_One_season), Lauda was again strong in his Ferrari; at midseason, he led the championship with 56 points while Hunt had only 26 despite wins in [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Spanish_Grand_Prix) (a race from which he was initially disqualified[^cite_note-henry32-42]) and [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_French_Grand_Prix). At the [German Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_German_Grand_Prix), though, Lauda crashed heavily, was nearly killed, and missed the next two races.[^cite_note-43] Hunt capitalised by winning four more Grands Prix giving him a three-point deficit going into the finale in [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Japanese_Grand_Prix). Here it rained torrentially, Lauda retired because of safety concerns, and Hunt sealed the Drivers' Championship by finishing third.[^cite_note-henry32-42] McLaren, though, lost the Constructors' Championship to Ferrari.&#x20;

In [1977](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1977_Formula_One_season), the M23 was gradually replaced with the [M26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M26), the M23's final works outing being [Gilles Villeneuve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilles_Villeneuve)'s Formula One debut with the team in a one-off appearance at the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1977_British_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45] Hunt won on three occasions that year, but the Lauda and Ferrari combination proved too strong, Hunt and McLaren managing just fifth and third in the respective championships. From there, results continued to worsen. Lotus and [Mario Andretti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Andretti) took the [1978](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_Formula_One_season) titles with their [78](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotus_78) and [79](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotus_79) [ground-effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground_effect_in_cars) cars[^cite_note-46] and neither Hunt nor Mass's replacement [Patrick Tambay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Tambay) were able to seriously challenge with the nonground-effect M26.[^cite_note-47] Hunt was dropped at the end of 1978 in favour of Lotus's [Ronnie Peterson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronnie_Peterson), but when Peterson was killed by a crash at the [Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_Italian_Grand_Prix), [John Watson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Watson_\(racing_driver\)) was signed, instead.[^cite_note-henry33-48] No improvement occurred in [1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1979_Formula_One_season); Coppuck's [M28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M28) design was described by Mayer as "ghastly, a disaster" and "quite diabolical" and the [M29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_M29) did little to change the situation.[^cite_note-henry33-48] Tambay scored no points and Watson only 15 to place the team eighth at the end of the year.&#x20;

Niki Lauda driving a red-and-white McLaren MP4-2

Five years after his first retirement, Lauda won his third title driving a [McLaren MP4/2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/2).

The 1980s started much as the 1970s had ended: [Alain Prost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alain_Prost) took over from Tambay[^cite_note-prost-49] but Watson and he rarely scored points. Under increasing pressure since the previous year from principal sponsor [Philip Morris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altria_Group) and their executive [John Hogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Hogan_\(motorsport_executive\)), Mayer was coerced into merging McLaren with [Ron Dennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Dennis)'s Project Four Formula Two team, also sponsored by Philip Morris.[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] Dennis had designer [John Barnard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Barnard) who, inspired by the [carbon-fibre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon-fibre) rear wings of the [BMW M1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_M1) race cars that Project Four was preparing, had ideas for a Formula One chassis constructed entirely from carbon-fibre instead of conventional [aluminium alloy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aluminium_alloy).[^cite_note-52] On their own, they lacked the money to build it, but with investment that came with the merger it became the [McLaren MP4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/1) (later called MP4/1) of [1981](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981_Formula_One_World_Championship), driven by Watson and [Andrea de Cesaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_de_Cesaris).[^cite_note-motorsportdennis-53][^cite_note-54] In the MP4, Watson won the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981_British_Grand_Prix) and had three other podium finishes. Soon after the merger, McLaren moved from Colnbrook to a new base in Woking and Dennis and Mayer initially shared the managing directorship of the company; by 1982, Mayer had departed and Tyler Alexander's and his shareholdings had been bought by the new owners.[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56]&#x20;

### 1983–1992: TAG-Porsche and Honda engines

Prost driving a red-and-white McLaren

[Alain Prost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alain_Prost), pictured at the [1985 German Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_German_Grand_Prix), won three Drivers' Championships with McLaren.

In the early 1980s, teams like [Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_F1), Ferrari and [Brabham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brabham) were using 1.5-litre [turbocharged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turbocharger) engines in favour of the 3.0-litre [naturally aspirated engines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturally_aspirated_engine) that had been standard since 1966.[^cite_note-tremaynehughes223-228-30] Having seen in 1982 the need for a turbo engine of their own, Dennis had convinced [Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Grand_Prix_Engineering) backer [Techniques d'Avant Garde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Techniques_d%27Avant_Garde) (TAG) to fund [Porsche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porsche_in_motorsport)-built, TAG-branded turbo engines made to Barnard's specifications; TAG's founder [Mansour Ojjeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansour_Ojjeh) would later become a McLaren shareholder. In the meantime, they continued with Cosworth engines as old rival Lauda came out of retirement in 1982 to drive alongside Watson in that year's 1B development of the MP4.[^cite_note-motorsportdennis-53][^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58] They each won two races, Watson won from 17th on the grid in [Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Detroit_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-motorsportdennis-53] and at one stage of the season McLaren were second in the constructors' championship. As part of a dispute with [FISA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9d%C3%A9ration_Internationale_du_Sport_Automobile), they boycotted the [San Marino Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_San_Marino_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-59] Although [1983](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Formula_One_World_Championship) was not so fruitful, Watson did win again in the United States, this time from 22nd on the grid at [Long Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_United_States_Grand_Prix_West).[^cite_note-60]&#x20;

Having been fired by Renault, Prost returned to McLaren once again for [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Formula_One_World_Championship).[^cite_note-61] Now using the TAG engines, the team dominated, scoring 12 wins and 2+1⁄2 times as many constructors' points as nearest rival Ferrari. In the Drivers' Championship, Lauda prevailed over Prost by half a point, the narrowest margin ever.[^cite_note-62] The McLaren-TAGs were again strong in [1985](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_Formula_One_World_Championship); a third Constructors' Championship came their way while this time Prost won the Drivers' Championship. In [1986](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_Formula_One_World_Championship), the [Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Grand_Prix_Engineering) team were resurgent with their [Honda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_in_Formula_One) engine and drivers [Nigel Mansell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigel_Mansell) and [Nelson Piquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Piquet), while at McLaren, Lauda's replacement, 1982 champion [Keke Rosberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keke_Rosberg) could not gel with the car. Williams took the Constructors' Championship, but for Prost, wins in [San Marino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_San_Marino_Grand_Prix), [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_Monaco_Grand_Prix), and [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_Austrian_Grand_Prix) combined with the fact that the Williams drivers were taking points from each other meant that he retained a chance going into the last race, the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_Australian_Grand_Prix). There, a puncture for Mansell and a precautionary pit stop for Piquet gave Prost the race win and his second title, making him the first driver to win back-to-back championships since [Jack Brabham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Brabham) in [1959](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1959_Formula_One_season) and 1960.[^cite_note-63] In [1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Formula_One_World_Championship) Barnard departed for Ferrari to be replaced by [Gordon Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordon_Murray) as Technical Director, with [Steve Nichols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Nichols) (who himself joined Ferrari in 1989) remaining as Chief Designer.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66] In the hands of Prost and [Stefan Johansson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Johansson), though, Nichols's [MP4/3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/3) and the TAG engine could not match the Williams-Honda.&#x20;

#### 1988–1991: Domination with Honda

Senna driving a red-and-white McLaren MP4/4

Equipped with [Honda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_in_Formula_One) engines and the driving strength of Prost and [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna) for [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Formula_One_World_Championship), McLaren dominated the season, winning all but one race. Senna won his first world championship after a season-long battle with Prost.

For [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Formula_One_World_Championship), Honda switched their factory supply to McLaren and, encouraged by Prost, Dennis signed [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna) to drive.[^cite_note-67] Despite regulations reducing the boost pressure and fuel capacity (and therefore, power) of the turbo cars, Honda persisted with a turbocharged engine.[^cite_note-68] In the [MP4/4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/4), Senna and Prost engaged in a season-long battle, winning 15 of the 16 races (at the other race at [Monza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Italian_Grand_Prix), Senna had been leading comfortably, but collided with back-marker [Jean-Louis Schlesser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Louis_Schlesser)[^cite_note-69]). At the [Portuguese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Portuguese_Grand_Prix), their relationship soured when Senna squeezed Prost against the pit wall; Prost won, but afterwards said, "It was dangerous. If he wants the world championship that badly he can have it."[^cite_note-70] Prost scored more points that year, but because only the best 11 results counted, Senna took the title at the penultimate race in [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Japanese_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-71][^cite_note-72]&#x20;

The next year, with turbos banned, Honda supplied a new 3.5-L naturally aspirated [V10 engine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V10_engine)[^cite_note-73] and McLaren again won both titles with the [MP4/5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/5). Their drivers' relationship continued to deteriorate, though, especially when, at the [San Marino Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_San_Marino_Grand_Prix), Prost felt that Senna had reneged on an agreement not to pass each other at the first corner.[^cite_note-74] Believing that Honda and Dennis were favouring Senna, Prost announced mid-season that he would leave to drive at Ferrari the following year.[^cite_note-75] For the second year in succession, the Drivers' Championship was decided at the [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_Japanese_Grand_Prix), this time in Prost's favour after Senna and he collided (Senna initially recovered and won the race, but was later disqualified).[^cite_note-76]&#x20;

With former McLaren men Nichols and Prost (Barnard had moved to the [Benetton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benetton_Formula) team), Ferrari pushed the British team more closely in [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Formula_One_World_Championship). McLaren, in turn, brought in Ferrari's [Gerhard Berger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerhard_Berger), but like the two seasons before, the Drivers' Championship was led by Prost and Senna and settled at [the penultimate race in Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Japanese_Grand_Prix). Here, Senna collided with Prost at the first corner, forcing both to retire, but this time Senna escaped punishment and took the title;[^cite_note-77] McLaren also won the Constructors' Championship. The [1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_Formula_One_World_Championship) year was another for McLaren and Senna, with the ascendant Renault-powered Williams team their closest challengers. By [1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Formula_One_World_Championship), Williams, with their advanced [FW14B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_FW14B) car,[^cite_note-78] had overtaken McLaren, breaking their four-year run as champions, despite the latter winning five races that year.&#x20;

### 1993–1994: Ford, Lamborghini and Peugeot engines

Senna driving a white McLaren MP4/8

By [1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Formula_One_World_Championship), Honda had withdrawn from F1 and the team used underpowered [Ford](30433662.md) [V8 engines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V8_engine) to power the [MP4/8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/8). Although Ayrton Senna (pictured at the [1993 German Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_German_Grand_Prix)) won five races, McLaren was not a match for the dominant [Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Grand_Prix_Engineering) team. After the [1993 Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Australian_Grand_Prix), the team failed to win a race until [1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Formula_One_World_Championship).

As Honda withdrew from the sport at end of 1992 following the [Japanese asset price bubble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_asset_price_bubble) in [Japan](15573.md),[^cite_note-79] McLaren sought a new engine supplier. A deal to secure Renault engines fell through due to being vetoed by Renault's fuel and lubricant supplier [Elf Aquitaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elf_Aquitaine), subsequently McLaren switched to customer [Ford](30433662.md) engines for the [1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Formula_One_World_Championship) season.[^cite_note-Henry_1999_pages=87–88-80] Senna – who initially agreed only to a race-by-race contract before later signing for the whole year[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82] – won five races, including a record-breaking sixth victory at [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Monaco_Grand_Prix) and a win at the [European Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_European_Grand_Prix), where he went from fifth to first on the opening lap.[^cite_note-83] His teammate, [1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_CART_PPG_Indy_Car_World_Series) [CART](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Championship_Auto_Racing_Teams) champion [Michael Andretti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Andretti), fared much worse; he scored only seven points, and was replaced by test driver [Mika Häkkinen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mika_H%C3%A4kkinen) for the final three rounds of the season.[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-85] Williams ultimately won both titles and Senna – who had flirted with moving there for 1993 – signed with them for the [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_Formula_One_World_Championship) season.[^cite_note-Henry_1999_pages=87–88-80][^cite_note-86] During the 1993 season McLaren took part in a seven part [BBC Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Television) documentary called *[A Season With McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Season_With_McLaren)*.[^cite_note-87]&#x20;

McLaren tested a [Lamborghini](18271.md) V12 engine ahead of the [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_Formula_One_World_Championship) season, as part of a potential deal with the then-Lamborghini owner [Chrysler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrysler), before eventually deciding to use full-works [Peugeot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peugeot) engines. With Peugeot power, the [MP4/9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/9) was driven by Häkkinen and [Martin Brundle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Brundle), despite achieving eight podiums over the season no wins were achieved. Peugeot was dropped after a single year due to multiple engine failures/unreliability which cost McLaren potential race victories and they switched to a [Mercedes-Benz](18564164.md)-branded, [Ilmor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilmor)-designed engine.[^cite_note-88]&#x20;

### 1995–2014: First Mercedes partnership

#### 1995–2009: Factory Mercedes partnership

For 1995 season onwards, McLaren ended their engine deal with [Peugeot Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peugeot_Sport) and started an engine full-works partnership with [Mercedes-Benz High Performance Engines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG_High_Performance_Powertrains) for the first time, after the German manufacturer spent one year in partnership with the then-youngest [Sauber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sauber) team.[^cite_note-89] The partnership included bespoke free engines from Mercedes-Benz that built, assembled and tuned by [Ilmor Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilmor_Engineering) in [Brixworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brixworth), [England](9316.md), Mercedes-Benz official team vehicles, financial support, also earned full-factory support from [Daimler AG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daimler_AG) and [Mercedes-Benz](18564164.md) and also Mercedes-Benz and Ilmor staffs would work with the team at their Woking base.&#x20;

McLaren's Formula One car for the [1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Formula_One_World_Championship) season, the [MP4/10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/10), was not a front-runner and Brundle's replacement, former champion [Nigel Mansell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigel_Mansell), was unable to fit into the car at first and departed after just two races, with [Mark Blundell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Blundell) taking his place.[^cite_note-90]&#x20;

Häkkinen driving a silver-and-black McLaren

[Mika Häkkinen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mika_H%C3%A4kkinen) (pictured at the [1999 Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Canadian_Grand_Prix)) won the [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Formula_One_World_Championship) and [1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Formula_One_World_Championship) Drivers' Championships with McLaren.

While Williams dominated in [1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Formula_One_World_Championship), McLaren, now with [David Coulthard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Coulthard) alongside Häkkinen,[^cite_note-91] went a third successive season without a win. In [1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Formula_One_World_Championship), however, Coulthard broke this run by winning the season-opening [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Australian_Grand_Prix); Häkkinen and he would each win another race before the end of the season, and highly rated designer [Adrian Newey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_Newey) joined the team from Williams in August that year.[^cite_note-92] Despite the car's improved pace, unreliability proved costly throughout the season, with retirements at the [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_British_Grand_Prix) and [Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Luxembourg_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix occurring whilst Häkkinen was in the lead. It was also at the start of this season that saw long time sponsor, Marlboro, shift its support to long time rival Ferrari. For the first time since the 1974 season, McLaren would have a new identity, shifting to fellow tobacco sponsor West. This saw the traditional red and white replaced with silver, grey, white and red. McLaren would retain this colour scheme (or very similar) for twenty years until 2017.&#x20;

With Newey able to take advantage of new technical regulations for [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Formula_One_World_Championship),[^cite_note-93] and with Williams losing their works Renault engines following Renault's temporary withdrawal from the sport, McLaren were once again able to challenge for the championship. Häkkinen and Coulthard won five of the first six races despite the banning of the team's ["brake steer"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4/12) system, which allowed the rear brakes to be operated individually to reduce [understeer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Understeer), after a protest by Ferrari at the second race in [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Brazilian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96] Schumacher and Ferrari provided the greatest competition, the former levelled on points with Häkkinen with two races to go, but wins for Häkkinen at the [Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Luxembourg_Grand_Prix) and [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Japanese_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix gave both him the Drivers' Championship and McLaren the Constructors' Championship. Häkkinen won his second Drivers' Championship the [following season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Formula_One_World_Championship), but due to a combination of driver errors and mechanical failures, the team lost the constructors' title to Ferrari.&#x20;

Mechanics pushing a silver McLaren into the garage

Mechanics pushing [Kimi Räikkönen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimi_R%C3%A4ikk%C3%B6nen)'s MP4-19 into the garage during qualifying for the [2004 United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_United_States_Grand_Prix)

In [2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Formula_One_World_Championship) McLaren won seven races in a close fight with Ferrari, but ultimately Ferrari and Schumacher prevailed in both competitions. This marked the start of a decline in form as Ferrari cemented their dominance in Formula One. In [2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_Formula_One_World_Championship), Häkkinen was outscored by Coulthard for the first time since 1997 and retired (ending Formula One's longest ever driver partnership), his place taken by [Kimi Räikkönen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimi_R%C3%A4ikk%C3%B6nen),[^cite_note-97] then in [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Formula_One_World_Championship), Coulthard took their solitary win at [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Monaco_Grand_Prix) while Ferrari repeated McLaren's 1988 feat of 15 wins in a season.&#x20;

The year [2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_Formula_One_World_Championship) started promisingly to coincide with Mercedes-Benz's 10th consecutive season in [Formula One](10854.md) as an engine supplier only, with one win each for Coulthard and Räikkönen at the first two Grands Prix. However, they were hampered when the [MP4-18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4-18) car designed for that year suffered crash test and reliability problems, forcing them to continue using a 'D' development of the year-old [MP4-17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4-17) for longer than they had initially planned.[^cite_note-boldnewdawn-98] Despite this, Räikkönen scored points consistently and challenged for the championship up to the final race, eventually losing by two points. The team began [2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Formula_One_World_Championship) with the [MP4-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4-19), which technical director Adrian Newey described as "a debugged version of \[the MP4-18]".[^cite_note-boldnewdawn-98] It was not a success, though, and was replaced mid-season by the MP4-19B. With this, Räikkönen scored the team's and his only win of the year at the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Belgian_Grand_Prix), as McLaren finished fifth in the Constructors' Championship, their worst ranking since 1983.&#x20;

Coulthard left for [Red Bull Racing](1173193.md) in [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Formula_One_World_Championship) to be replaced by former CART champion [Juan Pablo Montoya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Pablo_Montoya) for what was McLaren's most successful season in several years as he and Räikkönen won ten races. However, both the team not being able to work out why the car could not heat its tyres properly in the early stages of the season and the overall unreliability of the [MP4-20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4-20) cost several race victories when Räikkönen had been leading or in contention to win and also costing him grid positions in some qualifying sessions, which allowed Renault and their driver [Fernando Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Alonso) to capitalise and win both titles.&#x20;

In [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Formula_One_World_Championship), the superior reliability and speed of the Ferraris and Renaults prevented the team from gaining any victories for the first time in a decade. Montoya parted company acrimoniously with the team to race in [NASCAR](21114.md) after the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_United_States_Grand_Prix), where he crashed into Räikkönen at the start; test driver [Pedro de la Rosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_de_la_Rosa) deputised for the remainder of the season.[^cite_note-99] The team also lost Räikkönen to Ferrari at the end of the year.[^cite_note-100]&#x20;

[Steve Matchett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Matchett) argued that the poor reliability of McLaren in 2006 and recent previous years was due to a lack of team continuity and stability.[^cite_note-f1june2007-101] His cited examples of instability are logistical challenges related to the move to the [McLaren Technology Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Technology_Centre), Adrian Newey's aborted move to [Jaguar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaguar_in_Formula_One) and later move to Red Bull, the subsequent move of Newey's deputy to Red Bull, and personnel changes at Ilmor.[^cite_note-f1june2007-101]&#x20;

Alonso driving a silver McLaren MP4-22

[2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Formula_One_World_Championship) saw McLaren marred with controversy, including "[spygate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Formula_One_espionage_controversy)" and tension between the team's drivers, [Fernando Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Alonso) (pictured) and [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton).

After scoring no victories in 2006, the team returned to competitive status in [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Formula_One_World_Championship). That year saw Fernando Alonso race alongside Formula One debutant and long-time McLaren protégé [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton).[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103] The pair scored four wins each and led the Drivers' Championship for much of the year, but tensions arose within the team, BBC Sport claimed that Alonso was unable to cope with Hamilton's competitiveness.[^cite_note-104] At the [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), Alonso was judged to have deliberately impeded his teammate during qualifying, so the team were not allowed to score Constructors' points at the event.[^cite_note-105] An internal agreement within the McLaren team stated that drivers would alternatively have an extra lap for qualifying, however, Lewis Hamilton refused to accept for the Hungarian Grand Prix. Subsequently, the McLaren team was investigated by the [FIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA) for having proprietary technical blueprints of Ferrari's car – the [so-called "Spygate" controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Formula_One_espionage_controversy). At the first hearing, McLaren management consistently denied all knowledge, blaming a single "rogue engineer". However, in the final hearing, McLaren was found guilty and the team was excluded from the Constructors' Championship and fined \$100 million.[^cite_note-mclarenban-106] The drivers were allowed to continue without penalty, and while Hamilton led the Drivers' Championship heading into the final race in [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Brazilian_Grand_Prix), Räikkönen in the Ferrari won the race and the Drivers' Championship, a single point ahead of both McLaren drivers. In November, Alonso and McLaren agreed to terminate their contract by mutual consent, [Heikki Kovalainen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heikki_Kovalainen) filling the vacant seat alongside Hamilton.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108]&#x20;

Hamilton driving a silver McLaren

[Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) won [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Formula_One_World_Championship)'s season-opening race in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Australian_Grand_Prix) and went on to win the title.

In [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Formula_One_World_Championship), a close fight ensued between Hamilton and the Ferraris of [Felipe Massa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_Massa) and Räikkönen; Hamilton won five times and despite also crossing the finish line first at the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Belgian_Grand_Prix), he was deemed to have gained an illegal advantage by cutting a chicane during an overtake and was controversially demoted to third.[^cite_note-109] Going into the [final race in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Brazilian_Grand_Prix), Hamilton had a seven-point lead over Massa. Massa won there, but Hamilton dramatically clinched his first Drivers' Championship by moving into the necessary fifth position at the final corner of the final lap of the race. Despite winning his first Grand Prix in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), Kovalainen finished the season only seventh in the overall standings, allowing Ferrari to take the constructors' title.&#x20;

Before the start of the [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Formula_One_World_Championship) season, Dennis retired as team principal, handing responsibility to [Martin Whitmarsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Whitmarsh),[^cite_note-110] but the year started badly: the [MP4-24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4-24) car was off the pace and the team was given a three-race suspended ban for misleading stewards at the [Australian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Australian_Grand_Prix) and [Malaysian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Malaysian_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix.[^cite_note-111] Despite these early problems, a late revival had Hamilton win at the [Hungarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) and [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Singapore_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix.&#x20;

#### 2010–2014: Customer Mercedes engines

For the [2010 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Formula_One_World_Championship), McLaren lost its status as the Mercedes works team; Mercedes decided to buy the Brackley-based [Brawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brawn_GP) team that had won the 2009 titles with its customer engines, Whitmarsh having chosen to abandon their exclusive rights to the Mercedes engines to help Brawn run.[^cite_note-\:0-112] Mercedes still continued providing engines to McLaren, albeit under a supplier-customer relationship rather than the works partnership as before, while it sold its 40% shares of McLaren over two years but instead Mercedes remained supplying free engines for McLaren team until 2012.[^cite_note-\:0-112] McLaren signed 2009 champion, [Jenson Button](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jenson_Button), to replace Kovalainen alongside Hamilton in [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Formula_One_World_Championship).[^cite_note-Button-113] Button won twice (in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Australian_Grand_Prix) and [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Chinese_Grand_Prix)) and Hamilton three times (in [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Turkish_Grand_Prix), [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Canadian_Grand_Prix), and [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Belgian_Grand_Prix)), but they and McLaren failed to win their respective championships, that year's [MP4-25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4-25) largely outpaced by Red Bull's [RB6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_RB6).&#x20;

Button driving a silver McLaren MP4-29

[Jenson Button](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jenson_Button) driving at the [2014 Chinese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Chinese_Grand_Prix)

Hamilton and Button remained with the team into [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Formula_One_World_Championship), with Hamilton winning three races – [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Chinese_Grand_Prix), [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_German_Grand_Prix), and [Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix) and Button also winning three races – [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Canadian_Grand_Prix), [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), and [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Japanese_Grand_Prix). Button finished the Drivers' Championship in second place with 270 points behind 2011 Drivers' Champion [Sebastian Vettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Vettel) of Red Bull Racing, ahead of Hamilton's 227 points. McLaren was second in the Constructors' Championship to Red Bull Racing. Throughout the season, Hamilton was involved in several incidents with other drivers including multiple collisions with 2008 title rival Massa.[^cite_note-114]&#x20;

In [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Formula_One_World_Championship), McLaren won the first race of the year in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Australian_Grand_Prix) with a dominant victory by Button and a 3rd place from pole for Hamilton, while Hamilton went on to win in [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Canadian_Grand_Prix), but by the mid-way mark of the season at the team's home race at Silverstone, the McLaren cars managed only eighth place (Hamilton) and 10th place (Button), while the drivers' and Constructors' Championships were being dominated by Red Bull Racing and Ferrari, whose cars occupied the first four places of the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_British_Grand_Prix), this was partially due to pit stop problems and Button's temporary dip in form after not adapting as well as Hamilton to the new Pirelli tyres. The car also suffered reliability problems which cost the team and its drivers numerous potential points, including in [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Singapore_Grand_Prix) and [Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix), where Hamilton had been leading both races [^cite_note-115] and in Italy where the team lost a 1-2 finish when Button's car failed with fuel problems on lap 33.[^cite_note-116]&#x20;

[Sergio Pérez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_P%C3%A9rez) replaced Hamilton for [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Formula_One_World_Championship), after Hamilton decided to leave for [Mercedes](25098794.md).[^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118] However, from 2013 to 2014 seasons McLaren's [Mercedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG_High_Performance_Powertrains) links were weaker due to [Mercedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_F1) focused on its own works team. For eighteen seasons McLaren utilized bespoke free works engines, however from 2013, McLaren had to pay for Mercedes engines.[^cite_note-119] The team's car for the season, the [MP4-28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4-28), was launched on 31 January 2013.[^cite_note-120] The car struggled to compete with the other top teams and the season had McLaren fail to produce a podium finish for the first time since [1980](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_Formula_One_season).[^cite_note-MP4-29-121]&#x20;

[Kevin Magnussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Magnussen) replaced Pérez for [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Formula_One_World_Championship), and Ron Dennis, who had remained at arm's length since stepping down from the team principal role, returned as CEO of the operation.[^cite_note-MP4-29-121] McLaren was the first team to officially launch their 2014 car, the [MP4-29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MP4-29), which was revealed on 24 January 2014.[^cite_note-MP4-29-121] They had a largely unsuccessful 2014; their best result was in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Grand_Prix) where – after [Daniel Ricciardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Ricciardo)'s disqualification from second place – Magnussen finished second and Button third. Button subsequently finished fourth in [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Canadian_Grand_Prix), [Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_British_Grand_Prix), and [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Russian_Grand_Prix). Their highest grid position was in Britain with Button's third place on the grid.[^cite_note-122]&#x20;

### 2015–2017: Return to Honda engines

Two black-and-orange McLaren cars on track

The 2015 season was McLaren's first since 1991 to use Honda engines, but their performance was lacking and their reliability poor as the team finished ninth in the Constructors' Championship.

For [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Formula_One_World_Championship), McLaren ended their engine deal with Mercedes which included buying back the 40% stake that Mercedes held in the team and reforging their historical partnership with Honda. The Honda deal not only meant they would supply engines, but that Honda staff would work with the team at their Woking base as well as received full-factory support from Honda including official team vehicles and free engines. The team announced Fernando Alonso and Jenson Button as their race drivers, with Kevin Magnussen demoted to test driver. During pre-season testing at the [Circuit de Barcelona-Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Barcelona-Catalunya) in February, Alonso suffered a concussion and, as a result, [Kevin Magnussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Magnussen) replaced him for the season-opening [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Australian_Grand_Prix) in March. At that inaugural race of the season, Jenson Button finished 11th, but was lapped twice and finished last of the finishing cars.[^cite_note-123] Following considerable unreliability and initial suggestions that the Honda engine was underpowered relative to its competitors, steady performance gains eventually resulted in Button managing to score the team's first (four) points of the season at the sixth round in [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Monaco_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-124] By contrast, Alonso scored his first point three races later at the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_British_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-125] The [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) saw the team score their best result of the season with Alonso and Button finishing fifth and ninth, respectively.[^cite_note-Hungaroring-126][^cite_note-127] However, McLaren did not score points in the next four races until Button finished ninth at the [Russian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Russian_Grand_Prix). At the following [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_United_States_Grand_Prix), Button scored his best result of the season with sixth place. The team finished ninth in the constructors' standings with 27 points, McLaren's worst performance since 1980.&#x20;

Vandoorne driving an orange-and-black McLaren MCL32

[Stoffel Vandoorne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoffel_Vandoorne) in the [MCL32](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL32), showing the new papaya and black livery

McLaren retained both Alonso and Button for the [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Formula_One_World_Championship) season. The second year of the Honda partnership was better than the first, with the team being able to challenge for top 10 positions on a more regular basis. However, the season started with a crash at the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Australian_Grand_Prix) in which [Fernando Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Alonso) sustained rib fractures and a [collapsed lung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collapsed_lung) after colliding with [Esteban Gutiérrez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esteban_Guti%C3%A9rrez) and somersaulting into the crash barriers. Alonso, as a result of his injuries, was forced to miss the second round of the Championship, the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Bahrain_Grand_Prix), and was replaced by reserve driver [Stoffel Vandoorne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoffel_Vandoorne). Vandoorne scored the team's first point of the season with 10th place on his debut. The next points for McLaren came at the [Russian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Russian_Grand_Prix) with Alonso and Button finishing sixth and 10th respectively. The rain-affected [Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Monaco_Grand_Prix) was one of best races of the season for the team. Alonso finished fifth, having kept [Nico Rosberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_Rosberg)'s Mercedes behind him for 46 laps, while Button scored two points with ninth. At the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Austrian_Grand_Prix), Button recorded his best result of the season with a sixth-place after qualifying third in a wet/dry session. After the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_British_Grand_Prix) at Silverstone, the team scored points at the next three rounds with six points in Hungary, four in Germany, and six points again thanks to a seventh-place finish from Alonso at the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Belgian_Grand_Prix). At the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_Grand_Prix), McLaren matched their Monaco result with 12 points after Alonso claimed fifth position while Button once again finished ninth. Alonso and Button finished the championship in 10th and 15th places respectively with the team ending the season in sixth place in the Constructors' Championship with 76 points. On 3 September 2016, [Jenson Button](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jenson_Button) announced he would take a sabbatical from Formula One for the 2017 season. He then confirmed on 25 November that he would retire from F1 altogether with Vandoorne being Alonso's new Teammate for 2017.&#x20;

In February 2017, McLaren signed [Lando Norris](48978813.md) to the Young Driver Programme.[^cite_note-128] [Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Alonso) did not take part in the [2017 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Monaco_Grand_Prix) as he was participating in the [Indianapolis 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Indianapolis_500), being replaced by Jenson Button.[^cite_note-129] Button retired from the race on lap 57 after suffering terminal damage, having hit the Sauber of Pascal Wehrlein. McLaren finished 2017 9th with 30 points in total.&#x20;

### 2018–2020: Customer Renault engines

Alonso driving an orange McLaren MCL33

2018 wound up being Fernando Alonso's (pictured driving a [McLaren MCL33](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL33) at the [2018 Chinese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Chinese_Grand_Prix)) final season in Formula One, before he returned in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_One_World_Championship).

McLaren announced during the [2017 Singapore Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Singapore_Grand_Prix) weekend that they would split from engine supplier Honda at the end of the 2017 season and had agreed on a three-year customer deal to be supplied with [Mecachrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecachrome)-assembled [Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_F1) engines.[^cite_note-130] Team boss Éric Boullier described their performance between 2015 and 2017 as a "proper disaster" for the team's credibility.[^cite_note-131] [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Formula_One_World_Championship) was the first season in McLaren's history when their cars were powered by Renault engines. McLaren also announced that Fernando Alonso and Stoffel Vandoorne would remain with the team for the [2018 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Formula_One_World_Championship).[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133] On 6 November 2017, the team announced that Lando Norris would be the team's test and reserve driver.[^cite_note-Norris_Reserve-134]&#x20;

At the season-opening [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Australian_Grand_Prix), Fernando Alonso scored the team's best finish since the [2016 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Monaco_Grand_Prix) with fifth, Alonso said that the team's target would be Red Bull Racing.[^cite_note-135] McLaren had a relatively good start to the season with points finishes in the next four races, but in the next 16 races after Spain, McLaren only scored 22 points, 8 points less than in the same period in 2017. On 14 August 2018, Fernando Alonso announced he would not compete in Formula One in 2019, ending his four-year spell at the team.[^cite_note-136] [Carlos Sainz Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Sainz_Jr.) was signed as his replacement on a multi-year deal.[^cite_note-Sainz-137] On 3 September 2018, it was announced that [Stoffel Vandoorne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoffel_Vandoorne) would be leaving the team at the end of the season, with [Lando Norris](48978813.md) being promoted from reserve driver to replace him in 2019.[^cite_note-138] McLaren struggled with performance throughout the season, with the McLaren drivers being knocked out 21 times in the first qualifying session, and McLaren having the second-worst average qualifying ranking of any team, only ahead of [Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Grand_Prix_Engineering).[^cite_note-139] The team finished the season – after being helped by the exclusion of [Force India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Force_India)'s points from the first 12 races – in 6th place with 62 points, 357 points behind their target, Red Bull Racing, with the same engine.&#x20;

Norris driving an orange McLaren MCL35

[Lando Norris](48978813.md) driving the [McLaren MCL35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL35) at the [2020 Tuscan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Tuscan_Grand_Prix). Norris achieved his first career podium at the [2020 Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Austrian_Grand_Prix).

In the [2019 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_One_World_Championship), McLaren established themselves as the fourth-fastest constructor, behind Mercedes, Ferrari, and Red Bull. At the [Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Brazilian_Grand_Prix), Sainz recorded the team's first podium since the [2014 Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Grand_Prix), finishing fourth on the road but later promoted to third after Lewis Hamilton received a post-race penalty, meaning that the team missed out on the official podium ceremony.[^cite_note-140] McLaren ended the season in 4th place with 145 points, their best result since 2014 and 54 points ahead of their nearest competitor, Renault.&#x20;

McLaren retained Norris and Sainz for the [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Formula_One_World_Championship) season.[^cite_note-141] The season was significantly impacted by the COVID-19 pandemic. The season was shortened to 17 races, with the season opener to take place in [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring).[^cite_note-142] At the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Austrian_Grand_Prix), Norris achieved his first ever podium,[^cite_note-143] finishing in third.[^cite_note-144] Sainz achieved the team's second podium in 2020 at the [Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Italian_Grand_Prix), where he finished second.[^cite_note-145] The team finished the 2020 season third in the constructors' championship with 202 points.[^cite_note-Standings-146] Sainz finished the drivers' championship in sixth with 105 points and Norris in ninth with 97 points.[^cite_note-147]&#x20;

### 2021–present: Return to Mercedes engines

#### 2021–2023: Continued spells in the midfield

Norris driving an orange McLaren MCL35M

[Lando Norris](48978813.md) driving the [McLaren MCL35M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL35#Competition_and_development_history_of_the_MCL35M) at the [2021 British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_British_Grand_Prix). The 2021 season marked McLaren's return to Mercedes power since 2014; the team scored the only 1-2 of the season at the [2021 Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Italian_Grand_Prix), which was also their first win since the [2012 Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Brazilian_Grand_Prix).

McLaren again used [Mercedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_AMG_High_Performance_Powertrains) engines in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_One_World_Championship) after their deal with [Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_in_Formula_One) ended.[^cite_note-148] McLaren had previously collaborated with Mercedes from 1995 through 2014 (1995 to 2009 as a works partner and later 2010 to 2014 as a customer partner) but this time a customer role system by pay-lease agreement.[^cite_note-149] [Daniel Ricciardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Ricciardo) moved from Renault to partner [Lando Norris](48978813.md) for the [2021 Formula One World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_One_World_Championship) on a multi-year deal.[^cite_note-150] Ricciardo replaced [Carlos Sainz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Sainz_Jr.), who moved to [Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Ferrari).[^cite_note-151] In the season's first nine races, the team scored three podiums with Mercedes power, in [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix), [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Monaco_Grand_Prix) and [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Austrian_Grand_Prix), all courtesy of Norris.&#x20;

At the [2021 Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Italian_Grand_Prix), Ricciardo scored his first win since the [2018 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Monaco_Grand_Prix), and McLaren's first win since the [2012 Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Brazilian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-152] A second-place finish for Norris also meant that McLaren achieved their first one-two finish since the [2010 Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Canadian_Grand_Prix) and the only one-two finish for the 2021 season. Norris secured the team's first pole position in the hybrid era at the [2021 Russian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Russian_Grand_Prix) but was unable to convert it to a win, finishing in seventh place due to the sudden drastic change in weather conditions and team strategy in the last two laps of the race. A subsequent drop in form in the latter part of the season saw McLaren ending up fourth in the constructors' championship behind Ferrari.&#x20;

For the [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Formula_One_World_Championship) season, McLaren retained both Norris and Ricciardo.[^cite_note-153] Ricciardo tested positive for [COVID-19](63030231.md) ahead of the pre-season tests in Bahrain,[^cite_note-154] which meant Norris was required to do all the remaining running for the test[^cite_note-155] although a brake problem limited the testing he was able to conduct.[^cite_note-156] Both drivers struggled at the first race in [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Bahrain_Grand_Prix), with neither driver reaching Q3 – the first time since the [2020 Turkish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Turkish_Grand_Prix) – and finishing 14th and 15th in the race.[^cite_note-157] Norris achieved third at the [Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-158] After Norris missed the first day at the track during the [São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) weekend, McLaren suffered their first double DNF finish since Monaco 2017 as Norris had an electrical fault[^cite_note-159] and Ricciardo was involved in a collision with [Haas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haas_F1_Team)' Kevin Magnussen.[^cite_note-160] Ricciardo struggled relative to his teammate,[^cite_note-161] amid reports that McLaren could activate exit clauses in his contract.[^cite_note-162] This forced Ricciardo into releasing a statement on Instagram, confirming he would stay through to 2023.[^cite_note-163] In August 2022, Riccardo's contract for 2023 was terminated by mutual agreement.[^cite_note-164] [Oscar Piastri](59449712.md) replaced Ricciardo for the 2023 season after a contract dispute with [Alpine F1 Team](65240620.md) was resolved in McLaren's favour by the [FIA Contract Recognition Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Contract_Recognition_Board).[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166] McLaren finished the season in fifth place in the constructors' championship behind Alpine.&#x20;

Norris driving an orange McLaren MCL60

[Lando Norris](48978813.md) driving the [McLaren MCL60](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL60) at the [2023 Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Austrian_Grand_Prix). After a poor start to the season, the team rethought its design direction, resulting in an uptick in the MCL60's performance by the midpoint of the season.
